You sit on the porch and just feel a little sorry for yourself. But that's just life, ain't it? That ain't what you're talking about. You're talking about this... this cinderblock feeling. This two weeks or more of just staring at the wall, feeling like there ain't no point to putting your boots on in the morning. \n\nAnd if I'm being completely honest with you, doc, and I'm trying to be as honest as I can be here, I've never felt anything like this current hole I'm in. This is brand new territory for me, and it's terrifying, to be frank. Before all this happened, before the job let me go and I had that wild, fired-up spell, I was just a regular guy. I'd get up, go to work, come home, maybe watch a little TV or have a beer. I never had these long stretches where I just couldn't care less about breathing, let alone doing anything else. \n\nI keep trying to remember if maybe when I was younger, you know, when you're a teenager and everything feels like the end of the world... but even then, it was just a passing thing. You'd get mad, you'd mope, and then the next day you'd be out throwing a football around. It never stuck to me like this. This thing I've got going on right now, this absolute nothingness where I don't even want to eat or talk to nobody... this is the first time I've ever been dragged down this far, and for this long. It's just this one time, right after that high feeling crashed. So, no, I haven't ever had a two-week stretch of feeling this hopeless before now. It's all just hitting me for the very first time.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other episodes in your life where you felt down, hopeless, or uninterested in things for two weeks or more, before this most recent episode?\n\nPatient: Well, doc, like I was just saying a minute ago... no. Never before this. I know you're trying to make sure, but I swear on my life, before this whole mess started, I never had a spell like this in my whole thirty-five years. Never two weeks, never nothing. \n\nBut this one right now... man, since you're asking about feeling down and hopeless, let me tell you exactly what it's doing to me right now, because it's just eating me alive. Most days, I mean practically every single morning since I crashed down from that high, I wake up and before I even open my eyes, I got this... this heavy, empty feeling right here in the middle of my chest. It's like a physical weight, you know? Like somebody poured lead into my ribs while I was sleeping. \n\nAnd my brain just starts telling me right then and there that there ain't no point in moving. It's just this overwhelming sense that nothing good is waiting for me out there. Not a single good thing. No job to go to, no reason to even pull the covers off. So I just want to keep my head down. Literally, just keep my head down on the pillow, pull the blanket up over my ears, and hide. Because if there ain't nothing good waiting for me, why should I even try to face the day? I just lie there with that heavy emptiness, thinking about how I used to be able to just get up and go, and now it takes every ounce of whatever I got left just to breathe in and out. It's a miserable way to wake up, doc, just already defeated, keeping my head down so the world just leaves me alone.\n\nDoctor: Since you mentioned this current episode is the first time you’ve felt this level of hopelessness and lack of interest, have you ever had any other distinct periods in your life—before this most recent one—where you felt persistently down or uninterested in things for a week or more?\n\nPatient: Doc, I know you gotta ask these things every which way, checking if it was two weeks, or a week, or whatever number of days... but I'm telling you straight, no. Nothing like that ever happened to me before this whole nightmare started. Not for a week, not even for a few days. I was just... I was just a regular guy who liked his life, you know? \n\nBut this thing right now, this lack of interest you just mentioned... man, it's just stripping every last thing away from me. It's like... well, take the stuff I used to do to pass the time. I used to like walking by the lake. Just getting out there in the air, smelling the water, maybe watching the birds or skipping a rock or two just to clear my head. It was nice. And I had these little model kits I'd work on, too. Cars, mostly, you know, old muscle cars. I'd sit at the kitchen table for hours with the little tubes of glue and the tiny decals, getting the engine pieces just right. It gave me something to do with my hands, something to actually focus on and be proud of when I finished painting it. \n\nBut now... I don't know. Now they just sit there. The boxes are just sitting there gathering dust on the table, and I look at them like they belong to somebody else. Like some other guy bought 'em and left 'em in my house. I don't feel a single pull to go out to that lake, either. It's just water. It's just plastic pieces on a table. I don't care about none of it anymore. It's like the part of me that used to actually like doing things, the part that could find a little joy in a quiet afternoon... it just dried up and blew away, and I can't figure out how to get it back.\n\nDoctor: Since the time after you lost your job and felt that high, energetic state, have you had any other episodes where you felt similarly unusually good, energetic, or didn’t need much sleep?\n\nPatient: No, doc... no, nothing like that. It was just that one time. I mean, I keep playing it over and over in my head, trying to figure out what happened to me, but it was just that one single stretch right after I lost my job. You know, I got the news, and instead of getting knocked down, I just got... wired. Like somebody plugged me straight into a wall socket. I was going a hundred miles an hour, didn't need to sleep, felt like I could take on the whole world. \n\nBut since that crashed? Since that wore off? Absolutely not. I ain't felt a single drop of that energy since. Honestly, I mean, look at me now. I can barely keep my head up half the time, let alone feel \"unusually good\" or whatever. It's like that one spell just burned up every last ounce of fuel I had in my tank, and I've been running on empty ever since. \n\nI haven't had another burst like that, not even for a minute. No pacing around at night, no feeling like I'm on top of things, no skipping sleep. I wish I had even a fraction of that energy back just so I could stand up straight and maybe put those model cars together, but it just ain't there. It just happened that one time, and then... boom. Dropped straight down into this hole. So, no, to answer your question, there hasn't been a second time. Just the one weird spell, and then this endless heavy feeling I've been telling you about. It hasn't come back at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other times in your life, besides right after you lost your job, when you felt unusually good, extremely energetic, or didn’t need much sleep—even for just a few days?\n\nPatient: Doc, I feel like we're going round in circles here, but I get it, you gotta be thorough. But like I just said, and like I said before... no. Never. Not for a few days, not for a week, not for a single afternoon. \n\nBefore that one time right after I got let go from the plant, I never felt anything like that. I was just... normal. I mean, sure, maybe I'd have a good day if the weather was nice or if I found a twenty-dollar bill on the sidewalk, but nothing like that wired, buzzing feeling. I always needed my sleep, you know? Seven, eight hours a night, regular as clockwork. My old man used to say I could sleep through a freight train driving through the living room. \n\nSo, no, I never had a time where I didn't need to sleep, or where I felt like I was running on rocket fuel. It was just that one single stretch, right after the job fell through. And honestly, I wish I could say there was another time, maybe before all this, where I felt that good without the crash, but there just wasn't. My whole life, up until a few months ago, was just... steady. Wake up, go to work, come home, work on my models, go to sleep. Nothing extreme. No zooming around, no feeling like a superhero. Just regular life. And now... well, now I don't even have that. But to answer your question, no, never any other time besides that one.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Doc, I swear, we keep hitting the same wall here. There ain't no other times. It was just that one single time right after the plant let me go. But... well, if you're asking if people *noticed* during that one time, then yeah, they definitely noticed. 

Because right after I got fired, instead of being all worried about how I was gonna pay my rent or buy groceries, I just felt... I don't know, completely fired up. Like I was untouchable, you know? I walked out of that place with my pink slip and felt like I owned the whole damn town. I was talking loud, moving fast, making all these wild plans to do a hundred different things. 

But it wasn't just that I felt good. It was like... if anybody tried to step on my toes or tell me to take a breath, I would just snap at 'em. Fast. Like, my buddy came over and saw me out in the garage, just going a mile a minute, tearing through all my old boxes and tools, and he told me I should probably slow down and take it easy since I just lost my job. I was just so wired up that anybody telling me to slow down felt like they were trying to hold me back, and I'd get real mean, real quick. 

So yeah, people noticed a big change because I was acting so fired up and snapping at everybody. But again, doc, I gotta be clear—that was just that one time after I got fired. Never before that. I never had any other time in my whole life where I acted like that.

Because I remember looking at the calendar when they let me go, and it was right around the start of the month, and now here we are. It's just terrifying, doc, to think about how a man can go from feeling like he's ten steps ahead of the whole world, ready to take on anything, to just... nothing. Just a cinderblock on the chest. And it all happened in the span of a month. Thirty days of just swinging from the absolute highest high I've ever felt, where my blood was just humming, right down to this pitch-black hole where I can't even get out of bed. It's a month that feels like a whole year, to be honest. Just a solid month of my brain playing these awful tricks on me, throwing me up in the air and then slamming me down onto the concrete.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had more than one period in your life where you felt extremely high, energetic, or didn't need much sleep?\n\nPatient: Well, if I really sit back and chew on that for a minute... I mean, I'm thirty-five years old, right? You'd think if a man was prone to getting all fired up like a roman candle, it would've happened a few times by now. But honestly, looking back over my whole life, all the way back to when I was just a kid running around, or in my twenties trying to figure things out... no. I can't rightly say I've ever felt anything quite like that one time after I got fired. \n\nI mean, sure, everybody gets a little happy now and then, like if you find a twenty-dollar bill on the sidewalk or maybe you have a good afternoon with your buddies, but that ain't what you're asking about. You're asking about that untouchable, fast-blood feeling where you don't even need to shut your eyes at night. And I gotta tell you, doc, if I had felt that before, I'd remember it. It's just not the kind of thing a person forgets, you know? It was so out of the blue, so strange for me to be buzzing like that, snapping at folks and feeling ten foot tall. \n\nI keep racking my brain while I'm sitting here, trying to think if maybe there was another time, maybe years ago, but there just ain't. It was just that one single time. Before that, I was just... well, just regular old me, taking things one day at a time, never moving that fast or feeling that wild. So no, I haven't had any other times like that. It was just that one stretch right up until I crashed down into this hole. Just the once, and that was more than enough to turn my whole world upside down, I'll tell you that much.\n\nDoctor: Before this most recent period of feeling down and losing interest in things, have you ever had any other times in your life where you felt persistently low, hopeless, or uninterested in activities for a period of two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: Well, now that you're asking about feeling low before this... I mean, I really gotta think back on that. You know, it's funny how your mind works, how it just kind of glosses over things until someone sits you down in a chair and asks you straight out. But if I really, really dig back into it... no, I don't think so. I mean, I can't recall a time where I felt this heavy. \n\nSure, I've had bad days. Everybody has bad days, right? Like, maybe you get a flat tire in the rain, or you don't have enough scratch to pay a bill, and you feel kind of bummed out for a day or two. You sit on the porch and just feel a little sorry for yourself. But that's just life, ain't it? That ain't what you're talking about. You're talking about this... this cinderblock feeling. This two weeks or more of just staring at the wall, feeling like there ain't no point to putting your boots on in the morning. \n\nAnd if I'm being completely honest with you, doc, and I'm trying to be as honest as I can be here, I've never felt anything like this current hole I'm in. This is brand new territory for me, and it's terrifying, to be frank. Before all this happened, before the job let me go and I had that wild, fired-up spell, I was just a regular guy. I'd get up, go to work, come home, maybe watch a little TV or have a beer. I never had these long stretches where I just couldn't care less about breathing, let alone doing anything else. \n\nI keep trying to remember if maybe when I was younger, you know, when you're a teenager and everything feels like the end of the world... but even then, it was just a passing thing. You'd get mad, you'd mope, and then the next day you'd be out throwing a football around. It never stuck to me like this. This thing I've got going on right now, this absolute nothingness where I don't even want to eat or talk to nobody... this is the first time I've ever been dragged down this far, and for this long. It's just this one time, right after that high feeling crashed. So, no, I haven't ever had a two-week stretch of feeling this hopeless before now. It's all just hitting me for the very first time.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other episodes in your life where you felt down, hopeless, or uninterested in things for two weeks or more, before this most recent episode?\n\nPatient: Well, doc, like I was just saying a minute ago... no. Never before this. I know you're trying to make sure, but I swear on my life, before this whole mess started, I never had a spell like this in my whole thirty-five years. Never two weeks, never nothing. \n\nBut this one right now... man, since you're asking about feeling down and hopeless, let me tell you exactly what it's doing to me right now, because it's just eating me alive. Most days, I mean practically every single morning since I crashed down from that high, I wake up and before I even open my eyes, I got this... this heavy, empty feeling right here in the middle of my chest. It's like a physical weight, you know? Like somebody poured lead into my ribs while I was sleeping. \n\nAnd my brain just starts telling me right then and there that there ain't no point in moving. It's just this overwhelming sense that nothing good is waiting for me out there. Not a single good thing. No job to go to, no reason to even pull the covers off. So I just want to keep my head down. Literally, just keep my head down on the pillow, pull the blanket up over my ears, and hide. Because if there ain't nothing good waiting for me, why should I even try to face the day? I just lie there with that heavy emptiness, thinking about how I used to be able to just get up and go, and now it takes every ounce of whatever I got left just to breathe in and out. It's a miserable way to wake up, doc, just already defeated, keeping my head down so the world just leaves me alone.\n\nDoctor: Since you mentioned this current episode is the first time you’ve felt this level of hopelessness and lack of interest, have you ever had any other distinct periods in your life—before this most recent one—where you felt persistently down or uninterested in things for a week or more?\n\nPatient: Doc, I know you gotta ask these things every which way, checking if it was two weeks, or a week, or whatever number of days... but I'm telling you straight, no. Nothing like that ever happened to me before this whole nightmare started. Not for a week, not even for a few days. I was just... I was just a regular guy who liked his life, you know? \n\nBut this thing right now, this lack of interest you just mentioned... man, it's just stripping every last thing away from me. It's like... well, take the stuff I used to do to pass the time. I used to like walking by the lake. Just getting out there in the air, smelling the water, maybe watching the birds or skipping a rock or two just to clear my head. It was nice. And I had these little model kits I'd work on, too. Cars, mostly, you know, old muscle cars. I'd sit at the kitchen table for hours with the little tubes of glue and the tiny decals, getting the engine pieces just right. It gave me something to do with my hands, something to actually focus on and be proud of when I finished painting it. \n\nBut now... I don't know. Now they just sit there. The boxes are just sitting there gathering dust on the table, and I look at them like they belong to somebody else. Like some other guy bought 'em and left 'em in my house. I don't feel a single pull to go out to that lake, either. It's just water. It's just plastic pieces on a table. I don't care about none of it anymore. It's like the part of me that used to actually like doing things, the part that could find a little joy in a quiet afternoon... it just dried up and blew away, and I can't figure out how to get it back.\n\nDoctor: Since the time after you lost your job and felt that high, energetic state, have you had any other episodes where you felt similarly unusually good, energetic, or didn’t need much sleep?\n\nPatient: No, doc... no, nothing like that. It was just that one time. I mean, I keep playing it over and over in my head, trying to figure out what happened to me, but it was just that one single stretch right after I lost my job. You know, I got the news, and instead of getting knocked down, I just got... wired. Like somebody plugged me straight into a wall socket. I was going a hundred miles an hour, didn't need to sleep, felt like I could take on the whole world. \n\nBut since that crashed? Since that wore off? Absolutely not. I ain't felt a single drop of that energy since. Honestly, I mean, look at me now. I can barely keep my head up half the time, let alone feel \"unusually good\" or whatever. It's like that one spell just burned up every last ounce of fuel I had in my tank, and I've been running on empty ever since. \n\nI haven't had another burst like that, not even for a minute. No pacing around at night, no feeling like I'm on top of things, no skipping sleep. I wish I had even a fraction of that energy back just so I could stand up straight and maybe put those model cars together, but it just ain't there. It just happened that one time, and then... boom. Dropped straight down into this hole. So, no, to answer your question, there hasn't been a second time. Just the one weird spell, and then this endless heavy feeling I've been telling you about. It hasn't come back at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other times in your life, besides right after you lost your job, when you felt unusually good, extremely energetic, or didn’t need much sleep—even for just a few days?\n\nPatient: Doc, I feel like we're going round in circles here, but I get it, you gotta be thorough. But like I just said, and like I said before... no. Never. Not for a few days, not for a week, not for a single afternoon. \n\nBefore that one time right after I got let go from the plant, I never felt anything like that. I was just... normal. I mean, sure, maybe I'd have a good day if the weather was nice or if I found a twenty-dollar bill on the sidewalk, but nothing like that wired, buzzing feeling. I always needed my sleep, you know? Seven, eight hours a night, regular as clockwork. My old man used to say I could sleep through a freight train driving through the living room. \n\nSo, no, I never had a time where I didn't need to sleep, or where I felt like I was running on rocket fuel. It was just that one single stretch, right after the job fell through. And honestly, I wish I could say there was another time, maybe before all this, where I felt that good without the crash, but there just wasn't. My whole life, up until a few months ago, was just... steady. Wake up, go to work, come home, work on my models, go to sleep. Nothing extreme. No zooming around, no feeling like a superhero. Just regular life. And now... well, now I don't even have that. But to answer your question, no, never any other time besides that one.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other times in your life, even briefly, where you felt so good, so energetic, or so restless that other people noticed a significant change in your behavior, even if it didn’t last very long?\n\nPatient: Doc, I swear, we keep hitting the same wall here. There ain't no other times. It was just that one single time right after the plant let me go. But... well, if you're asking if people *noticed* during that one time, then yeah, they definitely noticed. \n\nBecause right after I got fired, instead of being all worried about how I was gonna pay my rent or buy groceries, I just felt... I don't know, completely fired up. Like I was untouchable, you know? I walked out of that place with my pink slip and felt like I owned the whole damn town. I was talking loud, moving fast, making all these wild plans to do a hundred different things. \n\nBut it wasn't just that I felt good. It was like... if anybody tried to step on my toes or tell me to take a breath, I would just snap at 'em. Fast. Like, my buddy came over and saw me out in the garage, just going a mile a minute, tearing through all my old boxes and tools, and he told me I should probably slow down and take it easy since I just lost my job. Man, I practically bit his head off. I started yelling at him, telling him he was just stuck in slow motion and couldn't handle that I was finally awake. I was just so wired up that anybody telling me to slow down felt like they were trying to hold me back, and I'd get real mean, real quick. \n\nSo yeah, people noticed a big change because I was acting so fired up and snapping at everybody. But again, doc, I gotta be clear—that was just that one time after I got fired. Never before that. I never had any other time in my whole life where I acted like that."
